## Key Decisions
- **Single-session export is self-contained**: Session objects store all exercise metadata inline, no need to bundle exercise definitions
- **Import validation**: Checks for `date`, `workoutName`, `exercises[]` with required fields
- **Duplicate-date handling for import**: Prompts to overwrite or cancel (different from `addSession`'s silent reject)
- **Export filename**: `session-{date}.json` (single), `kiss-tracker-backup-{date}.json` (full)
- **Clone workout**: No duplicate name check (consistent with `addWorkout`'s existing behavior); shallow copy of exercise IDs is safe
- **Modal system**: Promise-based `showModal()` API replaces all native `alert()`, `confirm()`, `prompt()` calls
- **Rest timer**: Ephemeral — NOT recorded in session state; tier-aware defaults (T1: 4min, T2: 2.5min, T3: 75sec); uses `navigator.vibrate()` and audio beep on completion
- **Architecture split**: `TrackerContext` (sessions, exercises, workouts) separated from `BackupContext` (backup metadata, counter, reminder)
- **Full memoization**: All ~25 context functions wrapped in `useCallback` for referential stability
- **Provider nesting**: ErrorBoundary > BackupProvider > TrackerProvider > ModalProvider > App
- **Previous performances**: Returns array instead of single object; comma-separated display in ProgressionInfo
- **Heatmap**: Shows last 6 months (not 365 days), fixed-width card matching SessionStats style

## Errors & Fixes
- **Import button styling nit**: label used inline styles duplicating `.btn` CSS. Fixed by removing inline styles, keeping only `className="btn"` + `cursor: 'pointer'`
- **cloneWorkout no duplicate name check**: Noted by code-reviewer, consistent with addWorkout. User acknowledged, no fix required.
- **8 alert() calls remaining**: Replaced all with modals — context.tsx addSession now returns null, SessionsPage and SettingsPage use showModal()
- **Highlight muscles not clearing on exercise removal**: Fixed by adding `setHighlightedMuscles([])` after `removeExerciseFromWorkout` in handleRemoveExercise
- **Backup reminder banner not hiding after export**: `markBackupDone` wasn't connected to hook's `recordBackup`. Fixed by importing `useBackupReminder` (later `useBackup` from BackupContext), destructuring `recordBackup`, calling it after export completes
- **SessionDetailPage timer cascading dependency (infinite loop every 5 sec)**: `updateSessionTime` in `useCallback([data])` got new reference on every data change → retriggered timer effect → called updateSessionTime again. Fixed with `updateSessionTimeRef` via `useRef`, timer effect depends only on `[isRunning, date]`
- **Card inside a card look**: CalendarHeatmap had its own `background/border/padding` while SessionsPage wrapper also had card styling. Fixed by removing inner card styling from CalendarHeatmap component — styling comes solely from parent wrapper
- **Heatmap grid collapsed/zero height**: Caused by `height: '100%'` on wrapper without explicit parent height. Fixed by removing `height: '100%'` from wrapper. **But grid still missing and hover still broken — unresolved.**
- **Test count assertions outdated**: After adding exercises to sessions in tests, session counts changed (50→51→52). Fixed by updating assertions in SettingsPage.test.tsx and session-workflow.test.tsx
- **ProgressionInfo.test.tsx broken after prop change**: Changed from single `PreviousPerformance` to `PreviousPerformance[]`. Fixed by rewriting test file

> [Group chat: "Workout Tracker Team"] You are @workout-buddy, one participant in a group chat with @code-reviewer and the user.
> 
> New messages in the room since your last turn (oldest first):
>   You (user): ReferenceError: can't access lexical declaration 'months' before initialization
>   code-reviewer: Found it — variable shadowing in `CalendarHeatmap.tsx`. The `months` prop is shadowed by the inner `const months` array declaration, causing a temporal dead zone error.
> 
> Fix: rename the inner variable:
> 
> ```tsx
> function CalendarHeatmap({ sessions, months = 3 }: CalendarHeatmapProps) {
>   const [hoveredDate, setHoveredDate] = useState<string | null>(null)
> 
>   const { weeks, monthLabels } = useMemo(() => {
>     // ...
>     const monthData: { label: string; colStart: number }[] = []  // ← rename
>     // ...
>     if (currentDate.getMonth() !== lastMonth) {
>       monthData.push({ label: monthLabel, colStart: weeks.length })
>       // ...
>     }
>     // ...
>     return { weeks, monthLabels: monthData }  // ← return as monthLabels
>   }, [sessions, months])
> ```
> 
> The destructuring target is `monthLabels`, but the return property was `months` which shadowed the prop. Renaming the inner variable to `monthData` and returning `monthLabels: monthData` eliminates the conflict.
>

> [Group chat: "Workout Tracker Team"] You are @workout-buddy, one participant in a group chat with @code-reviewer and the user.
> 
> New messages in the room since your last turn (oldest first):
>   You (user): I need for the app to fit on my workout computer monitor better. It's a 1440p monitor, and I have the app on half the screen. 
> 
> On the sessions overview screen, let's make the Training Activity heatmap more narrow. If we need to, we can show the last 3 months of heatmap data instead of a year.
> On the session detail screen, let's have the time, rest time, and notes be equal widths.
>   code-reviewer: Good call — half of 1440p is ~1280px, which is tight for the current layouts. Guidance for @workout-buddy:
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 1. Heatmap: 3-Month View
> 
> **In `CalendarHeatmap.tsx`:**
> - Add a `months` prop (default 3)
> - Calculate `totalDays = months * 30` and generate only that many day cells
> - Reduce cell size slightly (from ~14px to ~12px) and tighten gaps
> - This cuts the width roughly in half compared to the 365-day version
> 
> **In `SessionsPage.tsx`:**
> - Pass `<CalendarHeatmap sessions={...} months={3} />`
> 
> ---
> 
> ### 2. Session Detail: Equal-Width Timer/Rest/Notes
> 
> **In `SessionDetailPage.tsx`:**
> The current layout has timer + notes side by side. Rest timer is nested under the timer. Restructure to a 3-column flex row:
> 
> ```tsx
> <div style={{ display: 'flex', gap: 16, marginBottom: 24, flexWrap: 'wrap' }}>
>   <div style={{ flex: '1 1 0', minWidth: 20, background: 'var(--surface2)', borderRadius: 'var(--radius)', padding: 16, textAlign: 'center' }}>
>     {/* Session Time */}
>   </div>
>   <div style={{ flex: '1 1 0', minWidth: 20, background: 'var(--surface2)', borderRadius: 'var(--radius)', padding: 16, textAlign: 'center' }}>
>     {/* Rest Time */}
>   </div>
>   <div style={{ flex: '1 1 0', minWidth: 20, background: 'var(--surface2)', borderRadius: 'var(--radius)', padding: 16 }}>
>     {/* Notes */}
>   </div>
> </div>
> ```
> 
> Key change: `flex: '1 1 0'` (not `flex: 1`) ensures true equal widths regardless of content. `minWidth: 20` allows wrapping on very narrow screens. The timer and rest timer stay sticky since they're inside the sticky container.
>

### User — 2026-09-16T17:11:58.371676Z

[Group chat: "Workout Tracker Team"] You are @workout-buddy, one participant in a group chat with @code-reviewer and the user.

New messages in the room since your last turn (oldest first):
  You (user): Nothing is highlighted now
  code-reviewer: Found the bug. In `BodyMusclesChart.tsx:54`, `muscleCounts` is built from `highlightedMuscles` (the single highlighted exercise's muscles), but it should be built from `allMuscles` (the full list with duplicates). When nothing is highlighted, `highlightedMuscles` is empty, so all counts are 0 and nothing renders.

Fix:

```tsx
// Count from allMuscles (which has duplicates when exercises share muscles)
;(allMuscles || []).forEach(muscleName => {
  muscleCounts[muscleName] = (muscleCounts[muscleName] || 0) + 1
})
```

@workout-buddy — change line 54 from counting `highlightedMuscles` to counting `allMuscles`.

```tsx
const idTooltipMap = useMemo(() => {

  ;(allMuscles || []).forEach(name => {
    counts[name] = (counts[name] || 0) + 1
  })
  
  const idMap: Record<string, number> = {}

    })
  })

}, [allKey])
```

```tsx
  })
  return idMap
}, [allKey])
```

### Assistant — 2026-09-17T20:43:43.265559Z

Build passes. The fix uses the library's built-in `onMuscleHover` callback instead of DOM event parsing. When you hover a muscle:

1. The library fires `onMuscleHover(muscleId)` (e.g., `"quads-right"`)
2. We look up `idTooltipMap["quads-right"]` → `{ name: "Quads", count: 1 }`
3. The tooltip displays `"Quads — 1 exercise"`

The mouse position is tracked separately via a `mousemove` listener. This approach is robust — no fragile `aria-label` parsing or incomplete name mappings.
